# Development log

## October 14, 2026 — v001: fix Shelf payload request timing and accepted-drop lifetime

- Reviewed Apple primary DropDelegate/performDrop and NSDraggingSource documentation. Apple requires starting NSItemProvider loads within performDrop; old incoming-text receive deferred all loads inside a Task, violating payload access timing. Added ShelfTextDrop: synchronous request startup for all providers, lock-protected ordered collection, duplicate callback suppression, all-or-error main-queue completion, cancellable Progress handles and suppression even after completion has been queued. Inspector still checks operation identity before atomic metadata assignment.
- Fixed accepted internal reorder lifetime with a single-use receipt. Claim happens inside performDrop before token request; source-end clears only unclaimed session, preserving pending accepted load. Commit consumes matching receipt before token/model checks; stale/foreign/token-replay/invalidation/new-drag paths reject without metadata changes. View disappearance invalidates both active and pending state. Missing token now goes through a visible guarded error. No source/clipboard/schema change.
- Validation: 211 Swift tests and native bundle build pass; Rust unchanged at prior 47-test/Clippy baseline. Five new tests cover immediate-start count, out-of-order/duplicate callbacks and original item order, default NSString provider exact Unicode/CRLF, failure without partial document changes, cancel/queued result/progress behavior and accepted reorder after source-end plus replay/token/invalidation guards. Corrected an initial nonthrowing XCTest completion inference issue in the new harness.
- Fresh read-only QA state: same Open dialog on two checks with no manuscript open. Canceled it and quit isolated copy normally; prepared clean bundle, opened owned Checklist QA fixture and inspector Shelf, reviewed screenshot. Snippet-to-editor native gesture still gave no established source change; did not mark drag verified or attribute the unaccepted gesture to the payload bug. Escape canceled unfinished drag, then normal Quit. Final exact QA process absent. Prior diagnostic bundle was replaced; no diagnostic code reintroduced.
- Disk check: pre-turn exact manuscript bytes retained (Unicode/CRLF/omitted task/inline note), four Shelf items and foreign goals retained; no user manuscript touched. Release v001. Remaining: successful live in/out/reorder, source safety/Undo/Redo/reopen after real gestures, copy semantics/type/pin/cancellation boundaries and accessibility/light/performance/Highland acceptance. Full Shelf parity still incomplete; active goal continues.


## October 14, 2026 — v001: inspector Shelf and drag/drop implementation, live gate still open

- Added Draft/Shelf segmented inspector tabs, with label hidden visually for useful tab width. Shelf stays beside manuscript, offers searchable cards with direct edit/Manage controls and explicit native drag handles. Existing modal management and keyboard alternatives retained. Native drag payload contains exact content and opaque process token, without extra title/metadata.
- Implemented arbitrary before-target/end reorder with exact current identity/order guard, pinned-group boundary protection and preservation of foreign order slots/records. Incoming text drop loads every provider asynchronously and adds all snippets atomically, retaining exact content; cancellation plus operation UUID prevents late completion from a disappeared/replaced view. Filtered view disables drops/reorder with clear instruction. Outward native text drop relies on AppKit editor and still requires live acceptance.
- Validation: 206 Swift tests and final native bundle build pass. Three new tests exercise reorder/end/no-op/stale/pin/unknown-slot behavior, multi-item atomic failure plus all-category project reopen/attachments/foreign data, and actual native pasteboard writer text/token. Removed unused NSItemProvider outbound path so tests cover the writer used by the final native handle. Rust unchanged at earlier 47-test/Clippy baseline.
- Running QA inspector and native handles inspected; dark screenshots reviewed. No successful live gesture established: selected source drag extended selection rather than moving text; card attempts gave no state change. Replaced whole-card SwiftUI source with explicit native handle. Temporary diagnostic showed handle mouseDown/mouseDragged and native session start; target validate calls appeared with mouse-down initiation, but no performDrop and sessions ended operation 0. The logged validate value was the enabled flag, not a proof of type conformance; do not infer acceptance from it. Returned final source to conventional mouseDragged initiation. Cannot yet separate automation timing from application drop handling.
- Mac control tool reported user interaction on the attempted normal quit. Stopped sending UI input. QA process still live; did not force quit or replace it. Removed all diagnostics from source, reran final tests/build; prepare-qa correctly refused live replacement. Production target/Fountage.app is final clean build; running goal-qa copy is the prior diagnostic build and must be quit normally when safe before preparing the next build. Temporary event file /tmp/fountage-shelf-drag-events.txt contains only own event/coordinate diagnostics; no source content logged. Do not resume UI automation without fresh state and respecting human activity.
- Read-only disk check at end: QA source equals prior exact bytes, four Shelf records and foreign goals retained. Public version v001. Next: complete native drag acceptance (in/out/reorder), source safety, Undo/Redo/reopen/cancel/copy semantics, keyboard/VoiceOver/light appearance/performance and exact Highland comparison. This is implementation progress, not verified drag or full Shelf parity.


## October 14, 2026 — v001: atomic Cut/Copy to Shelf and durable native menus

- Audited Highland Pro 3.3.6 Edit menu on owned synthetic fixture: Cut to Shelf and Copy to Shelf present. Saved/quit reference, exact source ZIP verified unchanged. Added matching native Edit commands plus captured-selection actions in Shelf Add Item. Commands require editable single nonempty selection, no IME and project Shelf support; actual responder validation is repeated at dispatch.
- ShelfCapture validates exact source/bounds/surrogates/NUL before extraction, stores exact selected source as new snippet, derives title from first nonblank line (title only capped at 60 characters), and uses a document copy so failed creation cannot cut text. Cut removes only selected range and evaluates milestones in same final document assignment; copy changes only Shelf metadata. Blank selection content is retained. No pasteboard changes. Shelf cut refreshes insertion source/caret and disables another cut of the consumed selection; direct command preserves editor focus.
- Live verification found manually injected Edit commands did not survive SwiftUI menu rebuilding. First SwiftUI Edit fix then exposed old Format/Tools one-shot installation being dropped by focused-command updates. Migrated all existing Format/Tools actions to WriterCommands with scene availability and validated native responder routing, retaining existing shortcuts. Removed old one-shot Format installer. This integration fix is required to keep prior features reachable.
- Validation: 203 Swift tests and native build pass. Four new tests cover all eight category copy/cut exact roundtrip/assets/foreign metadata; whitespace fallback title; stale/invalid/surrogate/collision/unsupported atomic failures; native command validation/dispatch and guarded formatting routing. Rust unchanged at prior 47-test/Clippy baseline. Initial actor-isolation build error fixed by keeping capture/milestone workflow on MainActor.
- Live final isolated Checklist QA: Edit Cut captured [x] Review Zoë 🌙 CRLF [x] Send draft, source count fell 16→12; Shelf showed fourth item with exact text. Saved cut state. Undo restored original source and removed item together; Redo repeated cut, finally undone. Edit Copy preserved source and retained fourth item. Bold Cmd-B and Undo verified after menu migration; Format/Tools remained through source/selection changes and Tools numbered/marker contents inspected. Shelf Add → Cut Selection captured Revise the opening., leaving only checkbox; native Undo restored phrase and removed that fifth record. Saved/closed/reopened four-item state and screenshot reviewed.
- Disk: original pre-turn manuscript exact (CRLF, hidden task, inline note intact); copied snippet exact CRLF; prior Shelf items/pin/order/checklist and foreign goals retained. Highland reference source unchanged. QA quit normally, exact process absent, public v001 unchanged. Remaining: drag, broader Shelf widgets/grouping, full cross-window/modal/focus/IME/keyboard and accessibility/light-mode/performance acceptance. Neither full Shelf nor full menu parity is claimed.


## October 14, 2026 — v001: independent project Shelf notes, snippets and checklists

- Implemented Shelf core storage and native inspector entry across eight project categories: Note/Snippet/Checklist, title/content, pinning, explicit order, search, create/edit/delete confirmation and native document Undo. Checklist editor uses shared task controls over a local draft, with accurate draft-save hint and no misleading manuscript reveal button. Save commits metadata; Cancel leaves it alone. Shelf stays separate from source/counts until insertion.
- New version-1 shelf dictionary/order preserves future item records, foreign keys/fields and attachments. Unsupported namespace/schema/order disables mutation without replacing data. Guarded saves/deletes validate known-field snapshots and exact UTF-8 identity/content; new IDs cannot collide with existing/future/dangling-order records. No-op save avoids assignment. Move commands validate current identity/order and swap only neighbors within pin group, retaining foreign slots.
- Native editor now reports source+single selection; Shelf captures it at open. Insert uses a guarded ManuscriptEdit at that selection with exact content, preserving line endings and normal AppKit Undo/milestones. Stale source, invalid/NUL and split-surrogate ranges reject. New/edited draft insertion transfers text; Save separately retains Shelf metadata. Direct cut/capture/drag are not yet implemented.
- Validation: 199 Swift tests and final native build pass. Four new tests cover all kinds/category project roundtrip, exact source/assets/foreign fields, no-op/stale/delete/collision preservation, unknown records and schemas, pinning/reorder and native selection insertion Undo/Redo. Rust unchanged at previous 47-test/Clippy baseline. Official guessed Shelf KB URL was unavailable; prior installed Highland audit established independent Shelf checklist/new-item/editable text behavior; exact broad controls remain an audit gate.
- Live isolated Checklist QA: created pinned Opening revision snippet; document Undo removed item and Redo restored it. Insert replaced only selected Revise the opening.; Undo/Redo verified and finally undone. Created Revision plan independent checklist, checked continuity item, saved; created multiline Theme notes. Saved/quit/rebuilt final layout, reopened all three items with pin/completion preserved and unchanged 16-word source. Selected checklist+Return opened editor; Delete confirmation removed it, document Undo restored all content/state. Context Move Down reordered checklist below note; content search promise found only note. Final screenshot reviewed, Add Item compacted and singular count grammar fixed. Full VoiceOver not certified.
- Disk: exactly original pre-turn manuscript including CRLF/omitted task/inline note; all three Shelf records/order/completion/pin and foreign goals retained. QA quit normally, exact process absent. User manuscripts untouched, v001 retained. Remaining: cut/capture/drag, full Shelf widgets/grouping, light appearance, accessibility/performance and exact Highland comparison; no full parity claim.

## October 14, 2026 — v001: add and remove checklist tasks in the native panel

## October 14, 2026 — v001: checklist Return-key continuation and exit

- Audited Highland Pro 3.3.6 in the synthetic Navigator reference: Return after completed [x] First task inserted a new open [ ] item; Return on that empty item removed its checkbox and exited to plain blank lines. Native Undo restored all three audit edits to original source, explicitly saved restored reference, then quit.
- ManuscriptTextView now handles Return on a recognized task with one source edit: continue with an open checkbox and current indentation; on an actually empty source item, remove its checkbox and insert an ordinary line break. Existing completion state remains on the original task. Current physical LF/CRLF is preferred in mixed-ending documents, otherwise existing document ending is used. Each action has a separate native Undo name/group boundary.
- Nonempty selections, marked text/IME, multiple selections, noneditable text and non-task context retain AppKit’s normal path. Invalid/NUL/surrogate-splitting model inputs reject. Return within recognized screenplay notes/omissions/markers does not invoke continuation. A task containing only an annotation is not treated as empty source, preventing annotation deletion. Indented empty-item exit leaves plain blank lines; exact Highland indentation/mid-line policy still needs comparison.
- Validation: 189 Swift tests and final native bundle build pass; Rust unchanged at prior 47-test/Clippy baseline. Tests cover open/x/X continuation, mid-line splitting with original whitespace, empty exit, Unicode/LF/CRLF/mixed endings, hidden annotations/non-task/selection/bounds rejection and actual text-view Return Undo/Redo plus project reopen/foreign fields for all eight categories. No schema change.
- Live isolated Checklist QA.fountage: Return after Revise the opening. inserted [ ] with CRLF, second Return removed empty item. First Undo restored empty checkbox; second restored original source. Two Redos restored continued/exited state. Save/close/reopen retained four real tasks (three complete/one open) with no stray empty task and unchanged 12-word count. On disk, exact prior source plus only intended two extra CRLFs before omitted block; all hidden text, foreign goals, filters and output options retained. QA quit normally, exact process absent. User manuscripts untouched.
- Remaining: richer add/delete/reorder workflows, Shelf integration, indentation/mid-line/alternate Return/IME policy comparison with Highland, all-category live keyboard and full accessibility/appearance/performance acceptance. Existing task output/pagination comparisons remain open. Release v001 retained.

## October 14, 2026 — v001: markers integrated into the structural Navigator

- Added a shared derived NavigatorOutline combining structural rows and exact-range markers in source order. ## October 14, 2026 — v001: screenplay markers and native navigation

- Audited installed Highland Pro 3.3.6 using the synthetic Navigator reference: Tools → Markers contains Insert Marker, Insert Named Marker, Go to Next Marker and Remove Markers. Insertion produced {{%m}} and {{%m: }} at the caret. Undid reference changes before quitting. Official Tools KB confirms bookmarking and next-marker shortcuts; developer App Store notes identify the directive. No user manuscript modified.
- Rust now derives exact UTF-16 marker ranges/names for valid one-line directives, including inline markers and multiple markers on one line. Notes, omissions, escaped/malformed directives are not interpreted as markers. Hidden derived spans preserve original source and surrounding scene/dialogue classification. Valid marker directives are omitted from screenplay output and current screenplay word/character calculations; exact Highland marker counting policy remains unaudited.
- Added native Tools → Markers commands with Control-M insertion and Control-Option-M next/wrap navigation, named-marker caret placement and undoable removal with selection remapping. Insertion deliberately preserves selected manuscript text rather than replacing it; exact Highland selected-text insertion policy is not yet compared. Invalid/NUL/out-of-bounds/surrogate-splitting selections reject safely. Format/IME/multiselection guards apply.
- Navigator has a searchable Markers tab with named/unnamed entries and exact-range jumps using click/Return. Export Visible Markers produces a separate project from filtered directives; outline export strips hidden inline markers. Current tab layout differs from Highland’s combined Navigator; integration with folding/type filters and other writing formats remains open.
- Validation: 42 Rust tests, Clippy -D warnings, 170 Swift tests and native bundle build pass. Coverage includes hidden/escaped/malformed/Unicode/CRLF directives, repeated inline entries, counts/classification, output exclusion, next/wrap, selection preservation, native command Undo/Redo/format guard, export and exact source/unknown metadata/assets through reopen. A test initially used the wrong output API and an incorrect expected word total; corrected test assumptions. Invalid-range test exposed a Foundation trap on negative range and acceptance of a mid-surrogate position; added explicit bounds/surrogate checks before conversion, then all tests passed.
- Live isolated Script Markers QA.fountage: displayed two markers, selected inline Opening 🌙 exactly, shortcut advanced to unnamed marker then wrapped. Insert Named Marker and typing Ending added a third at the end without changing the displayed 10-word count. Remove Markers removed all three; one Undo restored all three. Search Opening showed one entry. Save/close/reopen retained three directives. Preview rendered the four intended content lines and no marker directive on one A4 page. Screenshots reviewed. On disk, every original CRLF byte remained plus the intended final {{%m: Ending}}; foreign goal metadata retained. QA quit normally and exact process absent.
- Remaining: combined Navigator integration, non-screenplay marker support, older/variant syntax, exact Highland counting/selected-text/blank-spacing/Undo comparisons, full VoiceOver/light-mode and feature-length marker stress tests. Full Navigator and marker parity are not certified. Release remains v001.


## October 14, 2026 — v001: preserve saved references during conflicting edits

- Saved cast/location operations now require the original typed snapshot for updates/removal. New cards cannot overwrite existing, malformed or future records with a colliding identity. Unsupported whole namespaces now disable reference editing instead of being silently replaced.
- Native details editor captures a stable original snapshot when opened, reports failed operations without dismissing the draft, and removes the saved original even if the form has unsaved name/notes changes. Final document-binding closures assign only a successful changed copy; unchanged saves do not assign the document. Notes still do not rename manuscript occurrences.

Tests cover stale updates/removal, deleted records, ID collisions, unsupported namespaces/containers, malformed individual records, no-op edits, exact composed/decomposed Unicode, unknown fields/records, source CRLF and assets through reopen across existing eight-category coverage. An initial new test compared the entire pre-save metadata against reopened metadata; normal save timestamps differ, so corrected the assertion to compare the entire reference record dictionary and source/assets instead.
- Live isolated Reference Safety QA.fountage: edited character notes with multiline Unicode; close-sheet Undo restored Original details; Redo restored updated notes. Removal with an unsaved changed name removed the original saved card; one close-sheet Undo restored updated notes. Save/close/reopen displayed the restored notes. Exact source bytes and complete known/foreign reference records verified on disk. Screenshot reviewed; QA quit normally and process absent. Final copy-before-binding assignment refinement was followed by tests/build, not a second full live walkthrough.
- Highland Pro 3.3.6 synthetic reference audit reconfirmed Navigator filters and Shelf item categories. It did not establish a cast/location rename command, so no rename parity claim or implementation based on assumption. Online guessed cast/location KB pages were unavailable. Richer reference fields, actual Highland behavior comparison, full VoiceOver, live conflicting-window/error-alert walkthrough and rename tracking remain open. Release v001 retained; no user manuscripts modified.

## October 14, 2026 — v001: reduce repeated page-milestone layout work

- Profiled a synthetic120-page screenplay with a pending page milestone. Baseline full edit/capture~50ms; cold layout~80ms. Added per-layout font and bounded256-entry fragment caches. Keys use exact UTF-8 bytes, semantic role, style runs and geometry; reused fragments retain occurrence-specific source lines. Cache lifetime is one layout, not a source history. No output styling/page policy change intended.
- Added Rust combined analysis/output snapshot and C ABI with shared allocation/free ownership. Workspace cache now reuses that same parse for editing indexes and page-milestone output, invalidating on exact bytes or format. No new document metadata/schema migration. Fast UTF-8 NUL checks retain existing invalid-source behavior.
- Validation:40 Rust tests, Clippy -D warnings,160 Swift tests and native build pass. Coverage includes complete combined-vs-existing JSON equivalence, invalid/null bridge inputs, single parse/index sharing, Unicode-normalization/format invalidation, latest-only cache and full cached-vs-disabled fragment/page/style/geometry/source-reference comparisons under Letter/A4/fonts/sections/synopses/continuations with long dual/Unicode material.
- Current-machine final observational timings: repeated fixture89,526 bytes/120 pages: parse8.94ms, disabled layout23.86ms, cached8.56ms, full edit33.88ms. Unique-prose99,186-byte/120-page variant:9.26/23.90/15.90/42.21ms respectively. These are individual diagnostic observations, not stable timing thresholds or Highland performance certification. Layout improved; whole writing pipeline still needs work to meet a16ms frame budget. One-pass engine sharing is structurally verified; do not attribute the entire observed gain to it.
- Live isolated QA opened saved120-scene/14,400-word fixture. Native key at end appended exactly a; save verified full prefix unchanged. One Undo and save restored original89,526 bytes. Milestone sheet showed120/1000, source and unknown metadata/pending status checked on disk. Full-document AX editor click was offscreen; native selection of the observed last heading enabled typing. QA quit normally and exact process absent. User manuscripts untouched.
- Remaining: typing/IME/selection/styling/JSON and simultaneous draft-goal latency, real manuscripts and comparable Highland measurements, all accessibility/production acceptance gates. Existing goal lifecycle and other-format output gaps remain open. Release v001, native Mac/Rust architecture, eight categories and branding preserved.

Wrapped dialogue lines now avoid isolated first/last rows within a longer speech, for single/dual dialogue and continuation-on/off output.
- Updated scene-heading keep lookahead to include intervening blank rows and the next paragraph. Regression caught the case where moving a three-row paragraph would strand its scene heading; the heading now moves with it. Explicit page breaks and source remain unchanged; shared PDF layout feeds preview/export/print.
- Verification: 115 Swift tests and native app build pass; Rust unchanged (38-test/Clippy baseline). Added 560 action-boundary cases across Letter/A4, 68 wrapped-dialogue cases, dual-dialogue exact text checks, scene-heading regression, Unicode/LF/CRLF, styled runs, forced breaks and source/project reopen. Rendered and visually inspected all three pages of output/pdf/paragraph-boundaries-qa.pdf. A QA fixture initially put whitespace before closing emphasis; fixed fixture to valid Fountain and added explicit retained-bold assertion.
- Highland evidence: official Print/Preview Preferences documents MORE/CONT’D but not full break rules (https://highland-kb.quoteunquoteapps.com/kb/preferences/print-slash-preview-preferences). Opened installed Highland3.3.6 and created a new disposable blank document. The tool reported user interaction before fixture paste/preview, so further UI input stopped; read-only state confirmed the editor was still blank. No existing manuscript was opened/modified. Highland may remain running with blank Untitled; do not discard any subsequent edits. Live Fountage QA and exact Highland comparison were not completed in this run.
- Limits: sentence-aware splitting, pathological long parentheticals, exact dual-column policy, full template/typography/accessibility and other output gates remain. No full pagination parity claim. Public release remains v001; manuscripts, branding, metadata and other writing categories unchanged.

## October 14, 2026 — v001: independent output typefaces

Family discovery is cached per process to avoid repeated expensive enumeration/sorting during feature-length layout.

New tests exercise role independence, generated cues, emphasis, changed wrapping, native menu target/actions/checkmarks, preference reload, unavailable-font fallback and exact source/project reopen. Initial tests caught hidden legacy Courier and unavailable Times aliases; menu/default handling fixed and tests use installed Times New Roman. Programmatic two-page PDF rendered and inspected.
- Live isolated QA: saved range fixture quit normally before preparing new QA build. In a new disposable document, chose Helvetica heading/Times New Roman body, exported Font Output QA.pdf, reopened preview and saved Font Output QA.fountage. Export inspection confirms embedded Helvetica and regular/bold/italic Times New Roman, with clean scene labels/indents/emphasis. Exact saved source matches original fixture bytes. Requested QA quit normally after saving; no production app/manuscript touched.

## October 14, 2026 — v001: undoable scene numbering and two-sided labels

- Researched the publisher’s Switching from Final Draft to Highland guide (https://quoteunquoteapps.com/highland-pro/library-files/Switching-from-Final-Draft-to-Highland2.pdf), Scene Numbers section: Add Scene Numbers appends sequential labels, rerunning renumbers, Remove deletes them. - Added native Tools → Add Scene Numbers / Remove Scene Numbers for the active editable screenplay. Rust now retains internal source coordinates on output atoms and computes one atomic minimal enclosing edit with individual patch coordinates. The Mac bridge remaps selections and applies through the existing native undo path. Already-correct numbering/no-scene drafts are no-ops. Replaced custom labels, omitted scenes, trailing notes, whole-heading emphasis, Unicode and line endings are handled without rebuilding unrelated source. Invalid input/overflow and unsafe internally marked/hidden numbers return an error before any edit.
- Printed scene labels now appear on both margins for Letter/A4. Long custom labels scale down to fit the left-margin slot and avoid overlapping headings or clipping the page; standard labels retain 12-point Courier. Escaped hash delimiters are no longer mistaken for printable scene numbers.
- Verification: 37 Rust tests, Clippy with warnings denied, 104 Swift tests and native bundle build pass. New tests cover exact add/remove round trips, custom labels/notes, omitted scenes, Unicode/LF/CRLF, idempotence, starting-number/overflow behavior, atomic unsafe-input rejection, cursor/range mapping, native format guard and one-step Undo/Redo, project and binary attachment reopen, both paper margins/PDF labels and long-label fit. Generated two-page and live one-page PDFs were rendered with Poppler and inspected.

Navigator showed #1#/#2#, counts stayed 9 words/2 scenes, and omitted scene remained unnumbered. Verified Undo restores A1/unassigned and Redo restores 1/2; Remove retains notes/styles, and Undo restores numbers. An extra automation Return keystroke after the first native menu click was undone separately before checking command Undo (Tools-menu clicks activate immediately, unlike the output popup’s AX selection path). Saved Scene Numbering QA.fountage/PDF and verified exact expected source bytes; exported private/omitted text is absent and both margin labels are visible. Quit only QA normally; user manuscripts and branding were untouched.
- Limits: selected-range/custom-start UI, arbitrary numbered non-scene elements, numbers with internal emphasis/hidden text, full keyboard/VoiceOver and exact Highland comparison remain open. The core supports starting numbers, but current native commands start at 1 for the whole screenplay. Unsafe internally marked numbers require direct source editing; no partial numbering is applied. ## 2026-10-14 — Slint persistent scratch recovery

Added per-session, persistent versioned source/category recovery snapshots under platform local app data using dirs 7. Atomic writes reuse the Rust core's tempfile/sync/persist storage helper. ## 2026-10-14 — Headless Slint editor acceptance and atomic source Undo

Previous turn was progress (persistent recovery implementation plus initial live snapshot evidence). Continued the revised Slint evaluation without desktop input. Added version-matched dev-only i-slint-backend-testing =1.18.1; no real windows, fonts, OS clipboard or native input methods are involved in these tests.

The first test of the actual generated TextEdit found that replacing selected Unicode text needed two toolkit Undo operations: first removed the inserted text while leaving the selected original absent. Confirmed the toolkit source calls delete_selection then adds a separate insertion undo item. Kept the original one-action restoration requirement and added a Rust source transaction history above TextEdit, storing UTF-8 changed spans rather than one full-source snapshot per edit. Source Undo/Redo keyboard callbacks now use that history. Recovery loads reset history so edits cannot replay into another draft; external source mismatch refuses replay. Main save-checkpoint/recovery/analysis callbacks are reused after history changes. Native context menu currently exposes cut/copy/paste/select-all, not an independent Undo path.

14 prototype tests, Clippy -D warnings and Mac build pass. The generated-editor test uses the same production history binding and exercises Unicode selection/replacement, exact CRLF preservation, single Undo/Redo restoration, callback notification, disabled input behind close overlay, select-all/replacement, isolated clipboard copy/paste/cut and atomic Undo of clipboard edits. Pure history tests cover Unicode scalar boundaries, empty strings/NUL, branching/redo invalidation, external-source guard and reset.

This proves logical editor behavior in Slint's simulated backend only. It does not certify physical Cmd/Ctrl key routing, real clipboard formats, native IME/preedit, bidirectional caret movement, actual font shaping, accessibility or large-document latency. History currently records one edited callback per transaction without typing coalescing or a retention limit; these remain acceptance work. Slint's public selection API uses UTF-8 byte offsets; the existing Rust navigation indexes use UTF-16 and still need an adapter before clickable source navigation. No Highland parity area certified complete. Current running apps and scratch work untouched; public v001 and paused heartbeat preserved.

Other native key events, modified shortcuts, explicit source selection, Save/Recover/About/category actions, Undo/Redo and close confirmation establish group boundaries. Replacements, paste/cut and multi-character input remain separate transactions; history stores exact changed spans with no source truncation. Not full Undo parity: mouse-only caret/selection changes are not directly observable through the stock TextEdit public callbacks; adjacent edits after those changes need native interaction acceptance. No broad parity completion claim. Public v001/heartbeat pause/running sessions preserved.

## 2026-10-14 — Slint outline navigation with stale-source guards

Previous turn was progress (typing Undo groups with generated-editor evidence). Replaced the prototype's static outline paragraph with native actionable heading rows. Rust maps the engine's heading line indexes into exact UTF-8 source ranges, preserving Unicode and mixed LF/CRLF and distinguishing duplicate labels. A navigation callback checks the row index, source snapshot, category and valid UTF-8 range before selecting/revealing the heading in the editor. It refuses stale rows with an updating status, and does not mutate source. The analysis worker publishes the matching outline source/category atomically within its revision-checked UI delivery. Programmatic source selection establishes a history boundary.

18 prototype tests, Clippy -D warnings and Mac build pass. Pure tests cover exact offsets after emoji, mixed line endings, repeated heading labels, invalid byte boundaries, out-of-bounds/negative ranges and stale source/category. The generated-editor test invokes the production navigation callback on the second duplicate heading, confirms source unchanged by navigation, replaces only that occurrence, and undoes exactly. It then changes source without replacing outline, confirms stale navigation is refused and verifies typing stays at its original caret position. No native desktop interaction or real clipboard access this turn.

The heading adapter uses engine line indexes, so this does not implement a general UTF-16 adapter for notes/markers/tasks. Native mouse/keyboard activation, actual scrolling/reveal, long-row truncation/visual layout and all-category interactive fixtures remain unverified. Sidebar button rows are functional prototype controls, not final Fountage visual design. Current running apps/scratch sessions untouched. Public v001, heartbeat paused; no Highland Navigator parity completion claim.

## 2026-10-14 — Slint project open/copy and full-baseline recovery integration

Previous turn was progress (exclusive package publication). Connected existing `.fountage` CLI input and Open Project…/Save Project Copy… controls to the shared Rust reader/new-copy writer. Project drafts retain original format/preset and metadata; the category selector is disabled, with a format-preservation explanation. Programmatic format change callbacks restore the project's category. Copy writes clone the project snapshot, replace only its validated source and publish exclusively. No plain-source fallback is allowed for a project copy. Plain source-copy NUL rejection now matches native export rules and is tested before file creation.

Before adopting a project, create an independent full recovery baseline package. Recovery schema 2 stores edited source/category plus project flag and resolves only the fixed sibling `project-baseline.fountage`; schema 1 source-only files remain supported. Missing/invalid baselines, category drift and project NUL source fail instead of dropping metadata. A recovered project establishes a fresh baseline/session. Open/Recover require current text to match its saved checkpoint before replacing the draft; close confirmation blocks their callbacks. Normal Save Copy updates the checkpoint only after success. Project save/close dialogs choose project-copy mode, retaining assets/opaque metadata.

22 prototype tests pass (21 logic plus optional render entry), Clippy -D warnings and Mac build pass. New workflow tests open an actual temporary package, edit Unicode/CRLF source, persist/reload project recovery, publish/reopen a complete copy with exact foreign metadata/Shelf-like data/binary attachment equality, and verify original entries unchanged. Failure test covers blocked baseline location, missing baseline/category drift/NUL and absence of source-only fallback. Category-code test checks persistence against shared engine codes. Raw metadata/timestamps/schema/identity remain unchanged; no type/schema migration, in-place save or conflicts. ## 2026-10-14 — Slint Replace Match and Replace All

Previous goal turn was progress (Find source navigation with native basic acceptance). Extended that partial workflow with an expandable replacement field, Replace Match and Replace All. Match action targets the last Find result, requiring the identical source/query/case snapshot; stale/missing targets reject rather than editing an old offset. All builds a new result from literal nonoverlapping matches in the current whole draft. Both reject NUL replacements, support empty deletion and literal dollar/backslash text, guard the editor byte-offset size, and do nothing behind close confirmation. After an action the old target is invalidated, preventing accidental replay. Identical replacements create no edit/history/recovery entry.

## 2026-10-14 — Regex Find and validated capture replacement

Previous turn was progress (whole-word search). Continued partially implemented Find workflow. Read primary developer reference https://johnaugust.com/2014/find-and-replace-a-screenwriters-best-friends : historical Highland supports wildcard tokens backed by regular expressions, including bracketed-notes→bold conversion. This is not proof of current Pro regex syntax/UI parity. Consulted regex1.13.1 capture semantics at https://docs.rs/regex/latest/regex/struct.Captures.html .

Added opt-in Regular expression option to shared Find options; ordinary searches/replacements remain escaped/literal, including $1/backslashes. Pattern builder shares case/word options, 2MiB compiled-size guard and explicit invalid/unsupported-pattern feedback (look-around/backreferences unsupported by Rust engine). UI options changes invalidate old replacement target. Regex replacements support $0/$1/${1}/$name/${name} and $$; parser resolves references against the compiled expression before searching. Unknown/overflowing references and unclosed braces reject the entire action; unmatched optional groups expand empty. Backslashes stay literal. Per-piece/output size checks preserve editor i32 bounds. No partial source update on failure. Literal replacement retains find_iter/no-capture fast path; Replace Match skips only to selected occurrence, never scans the remainder unnecessarily. Captures refer to exact original source, not progressively replaced text. Regex find/replace can visit zero-width Unicode-boundary matches finitely; empty query still requires user input. No original-file mutation.

Expanded Find rows and replacement capture hint to fit compact width. Explicit11-state render suite passes and compact dark Replace image inspected: controls/status/hint fit without overlap; source remains usable with less vertical space. Core tests cover named/numbered/whole-match captures, escaped dollars/literal backslashes, optional missing group, invalid expressions/unsupported lookahead, bad references, zero-width multiline navigation/replace and capture numbering with Whole word wrapper. New continuation API revalidates exact new source/pattern/options and finds the first match beginning after the inserted span. It skips newly inserted matching text until wrap, includes an adjacent match moved to the same offset by deletion, and skips the relocated same zero-width match when replacing zero-width patterns. Retains proper new match ordinal/count for the next Replace action. No-wrap reports end; wrapping falls back to first remaining match. No remaining matches disables Replace/Replace&Find. Whole-source linear scan per continuation; large-input responsiveness still unmeasured.

Core test checks insertion of matching catcat skips both inserted matches, next replacement uses the correct old remainder, deletion of adjacent cat retains next match at same byte offset, empty regex replacement progresses to next Unicode-boundary line, final zero-width insertion respects no-wrap, and invalid UTF8 continuation offset rejects. ## 2026-10-15 — Shaped keyboard navigation and caret affinity

Previous goal turn was progress: stateful formatted-editor transactions, exact save/reopen and isolation checks were implemented. Added actual shaped keyboard motion to that controller. New Position carries original UTF8 byte plus upstream/downstream affinity. Document retains the backward-compatible caret(byte)/hit_test APIs, and adds affinity-aware caret_at/hit_position, horizontal visual cluster motion, vertical motion over shaped lines/paragraphs, and shaped line boundaries. Uses public Parley cursor/selection and line metrics APIs (an initial attempt to use private line_for_offset was replaced with public line iteration). Glyph renderer now draws the requested caret affinity; CLI command/report supports move(direction,extend), position affinity and caretUpstream metrics.

Editor tracks anchor/focus affinities and a preferred global x for repeated Up/Down through short lines and paragraphs with different indents. Left/Right follow shaped visual cluster order and skip internal Unicode grapheme positions, rather than incrementing bytes; crossing a soft wrap preserves two distinct visual positions at the same source byte. Horizontal collapse of a nonempty reversed selection chooses its visible endpoint. Shift movement retains anchor, document/line bounds use original ranges, and pointer placement retains the actual shaped cursor affinity. Editing, pointer placement, explicit selection and width reflow reset the remembered vertical column appropriately. Selection/motion commands create no source edit or history transaction; subsequent replacement still uses shared Undo/Redo.

## 2026-10-15 — Composition transaction and display groundwork (native delivery still open)

Previous goal turn was progress: paragraph-shape reuse/rewrapping, viewport bounds and native long-draft exact save/reopen were implemented. Investigated the next input-method gate using pinned Slint1.18.1 source and current official TextInput/FocusScope/WindowEvent documentation. Backend InternalKeyEvent carries UpdateComposition/CommitComposition, preedit text/selection, signed replacement range and cursor/anchor fields. InternalEvent.public_representation explicitly excludes composition, so the public window event hook does not deliver it. FocusScope has ordinary key callbacks, not a composition callback. InputMethodRequest/Properties (enable/update/disable, surrounding text and candidate cursor rectangle) live in the internal window-adapter contract. The new code below therefore does not claim native IME delivery or candidate positioning; no private runtime dependency, registry edit or toolkit fork was introduced this turn.

Implemented editor_composition::Marked as a separate immutable display document. Committed manuscript source/selection remain authoritative and untouched by updates. Preedit stages replacement against committed original UTF8 ranges, shapes its own provisional source with existing reuse, tracks cursor/selection and paints marked underlines. Visual endpoints normalize to grapheme/shaped-caret boundaries, including combining accents joining existing source. Added controller Preedit, CommitComposition and CancelComposition commands. Explicit absolute replacement ranges support reconversion groundwork; they are not yet a mapping of native relative/signed range units. Empty Preedit cancels; explicit CommitComposition performs replacement, including empty text when intentionally requested. Native empty-event interpretation still requires the backend bridge.

Repeated updates do not record History or alter source. Commit changes source once; Cancel restores the original display/selection, and Undo while composing cancels before undoing earlier committed edits. Identical-text commit collapses caret without manufacturing a source edit. Stale generation, invalid UTF8/cursor/replacement and ordinary source-edit commands during composition are rejected without destroying pending state. Width reflow stages committed and preview documents before adopting either; failed reflow/adoption leaves source/pending state intact. Successful external adoption explicitly cancels composition. These are source/editor transaction guarantees, not full IME acceptance.

Live adapter now has explicit preedit/commit/cancel callbacks for a future native bridge. Renderer uses display-document coordinates for composition/caret/scroll while fallback/clipboard always retain committed-source coordinates. Copy/Cut selection availability is suppressed while composing. Source updates/dirty/history/recovery only occur on commit. Mode exit/focus helper cancels preview in this prototype; that policy is provisional and must be reconciled with platform/Highland focus/save behavior when the native input context is connected. Added composing-caption feedback. Initial tests exposed missing cancellation on external adoption and mode-exit focus helper; fixed both. Existing ordinary editor behavior remains the default.

Validation:64prototype tests (+3 composition transaction tests and +1 actual generated-widget adapter-callback test),29software-render states (+2 Japanese marked-text light/dark/compact states), prototype all-target Clippy with warnings denied and Mac bundle build pass. Tests verify reversed selection, multiple Japanese updates, no source/history/recovery changes during preview, exact save/reopen while preview is visible, cancellation, single source edit on commit, UndoRedo, unchanged-text commit, combining marks, invalid/stale events, blocked ordinary edit against provisional text, reflow/adoption and normal input after leaving the mode. Generated-widget callbacks are injected directly: they are not a physical input method or backend event test. Inspected marked Japanese underline/caret in both paper modes.

Evidence output/slint-composition-20261015/: CLI --edit-layout preview, commit and cancel command sequences, source-copy/SVG/PNG/metrics for each,29workspace renders and verification.json. Preview paints marked text yet saves exact original source with0source-changing commands; cancellation also saves original; Japanese commit changes source exactly once and clears mark. Original QA fixture remains unchanged. No user's running app or manuscripts were touched; no native IME end-to-end claim.

Remaining/native bridge requirements: transport actual backend composition and input-method enable/update/disable requests without stealing standard TextInput or file-picker focus; convert signed relative replacement and selection units explicitly; surrounding-text/candidate rectangle follows actual formatted caret, scroll, window origin and scale; process partial commit with continuing preedit atomically; resolve platform focus/save/close/reconversion semantics; verify physical Japanese/Chinese/Korean/dead-key inputs, caret/selection, undo/save/reopen, picker isolation and all platforms. A transparent plain TextInput overlay alone would misplace formatted candidate geometry and is not accepted as completion. Accessible text remains independently open. Public v001/branding/eight categories/paused heartbeat preserved; goal remains active.

## 2026-10-15 — Metadata save checkpoints, isolated history and durable recovery

Previous goal turn was progress: shared independent Shelf transactions and Rust/Swift interoperability. Extended Slint Checkpoint to track current/saved metadata values alongside exact source. mark_saved records both; Undoing saved metadata remains dirty and Redo back to saved metadata becomes clean. New-project, startup project, Open Project, Open Source and Recover adoption paths now set/clear metadata appropriately; source-only files cannot inherit a previous project's metadata checkpoint. Recovered drafts still require explicit Save Copy, even when source matches a prior checkpoint. Existing Close/Quit/new/open guards now honor metadata dirty state through the same is_clean policy.

New project_metadata::History owns raw before/after info.json bytes only, not stale manuscripts or full assets. Guards current project ID/raw metadata/checkpoint before applying history; Undo/Redo receive live manuscript source and retain unrelated later text. Shelf edit/no-op/stale validation uses shared Project API. Each successful metadata edit or history action publishes a complete immutable Recovery baseline and snapshot first; only after success does it adopt project/checkpoint/history. Failure preserves all prior memory and history state; no-op writes nothing. Branching clears Redo only after successful publication. Raw info.json restoration retains original byte formatting. History API is ready for controller integration; no editable Shelf UI is enabled yet.

## 2026-10-15 — Ordered native Shelf shortcuts and close-boundary history

Previous goal turn was progress: direct task entry, native save/reopen and the discovery that a rapid Redo/Escape/Open batch could cancel Redo before it restored the saved checkpoint. Addressed this known reliability defect before adding further Shelf controls. The native reproduction now passes: accepted Shelf Redo finishes before Escape closes the panel, and a following Open Project command sees the clean saved state. This supersedes the specific cancellation limitation recorded in the preceding entry; it does not certify all native command combinations.

AppKit admission now records intercepted Shelf history/focus/clipboard/apply intents in a Rust-only queue without UI mutation, parsing or disk IO. The controller drains in order at the next UI wakeup and before close, form save/cancel, typing, clipboard boundaries and owned focus transitions. Admission captures project identity, panel/form session and context; resets clear pending work, and stale commands cannot target an adopted document or a new form (including same-ID copies). Multiple wakeups cannot replay commands. A reentrancy guard permits normal focus/history callbacks during a drain without recursively consuming the queue.

Native Tabs join the same queue. Later field history/clipboard commands target the logical resulting control instead of the old field still visible before dispatch. Clipboard operations can address their admitted field without stealing later focus. Queued field Undo updates its original field if a later pointer focus has moved elsewhere. Marked-text-blocked intent stays blocked even if composition commits before dispatch. Disabled manuscript formatting/note chords are consumed in plain Shelf forms so they cannot become literal letters after lost Command modifiers. Other app editing surfaces retain their existing dispatch paths.

A failed command stops the remaining batch with an explicit error, preserves the form/panel and the current project/checkpoint/recovery/history state for that failed action, and prevents a close/save transition from hiding the failure. Already completed earlier commands are not rolled back: this is ordered execution, not an atomic multi-command transaction. Later queued commands are reported as not applied, rather than silently executing against a failed predecessor. Explicit retry after the recovery fault clears succeeds; late empty wakeups do not erase the error.

Validation: 141 prototype tests pass, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, fmt check and Mac bundle build pass. Eight new regressions cover accepted Redo before immediate close across all eight categories, multiple FIFO history actions with no replay, exact saved/recovered project state, field Undo before save or later focus, Tab/history/clipboard/apply ordering, preserving later pointer focus, failure blocking close with unchanged recovery/checkpoint, pending-apply validation failure, same-ID document reset invalidation and an IME-blocked intent after commit. Native admission tests verify no recovery callback/IO is invoked until drain. Existing 133 editor/checklist/form/metadata tests remain passing. No layout styling change required new visual renders.

Native Mac: loaded isolated Native Shelf Tasks project, toggled one completion state and saved Native Shelf Shortcut Order.fountage. Undo away from saved checkpoint showed Edited. A rapid Shift-Command-Z/Escape batch closed Shelf with Edited cleared. Repeated Undo and rapid Shift-Command-Z/Escape/Command-O opened the native picker directly, with no dirty-project refusal. These are the same sequences that failed previously. Manuscript display remained exact. The Mac-control tool then reported external app interaction while selecting a copy to reopen; no further UI actions were issued, preserving the user's control. Additional native draft-field Tab/history/clipboard/apply and disabled-formatting-chord checks were therefore not completed this turn; generated-window coverage is not a substitute for that remaining native matrix.

Evidence: output/slint-shelf-shortcut-order-20261015/verification.json records full metadata, package entry hashes, native observations and uncompleted native checks. Native Shelf Shortcut Order.fountage differs from Native Shelf Tasks.fountage only by the intended first checkbox completion character. All other metadata, source bytes, binary attachment and symlink target remain exact. A matching complete recovery baseline/snapshot is archived under native-recovery. No user manuscript or original fixture changed.

Remaining: additional native field/clipboard/apply/IME/pointer timing combinations, cross-context chronological Undo and transitions into newly created/closed forms, direct task edit/removal/reordering, checklist management, notes/snippets, Highland Shelf compatibility, large-project recovery performance, wider accessibility and Windows/Linux gates. This fixes the observed native Shelf history/close race; full keyboard, Shelf and Highland parity remain unproven. v001 unchanged; heartbeat paused; goal active.

## 2026-10-15 — Checklist pinning, independent pin order and return-to-place

Previous goal turn was progress: task ordering with a fixed native repeated-Return focus defect and package evidence. Continued the remaining checklist-management workflow. Rechecked the developer’s current App Store release notes (https://apps.apple.com/kz/app/highland-pro/id6612007609?platform=mac, accessed 2026-10-15), which explicitly describe pinning Shelf items, rearranging pins and unpinning back to their former place. That requirement exposed a limitation in the existing Fountage order model: moving pinned items rewrote normal order. Implemented independent pin order in both shared Rust and the Swift reference, rather than treating approximate pinned grouping as complete. Exact Highland drag/context-menu interaction equivalence is still not certified.

The Shelf version-1 record now optionally stores unique-string pinnedOrder independently of normal order. Legacy records retain their prior view until pins are rearranged. Pin/unpin changes the item flag; pinned moves write pinnedOrder without changing normal order; unpinning therefore returns the item to its base position even after rearrangement. Unknown/reserved identities and opaque records remain intact. Both readers reject malformed pinnedOrder for Shelf writes; Add reserves identities in both order arrays, and removing a known item cleans only that identity. Swift read/move/delete and validation support the same field. Document/public versions remain unchanged. Details are in docs/FILE_FORMAT.md.

Slint now exposes accessible Pin/Unpin and checklist Up/Down controls, pinned/normal group headings, disabled group boundaries and keyboard traversal. A MoveWithinKind operation skips invisible notes/snippets while preserving their order-array slots, unknown entries and full expected-order checks. Pin/move changes publish complete recovery before adoption and use metadata undo/redo. Stale snapshots, pending Undo changing the view, active forms and blocked recovery do not adopt writes or discard field text. Structural checklist moves return focus to stable Done, avoiding the destroyed-row focus problem. Existing task operations and directed manuscript selection remain intact.

Validation: 85 core tests and 150 Slint tests pass, with light/dark compact/wide/pinned/long-title renders inspected under output/slint-checklist-management-20261015/verified. Root library/test and Slint all-target Clippy with warnings denied, fmt checks and Mac bundle build pass. Full Swift suite passes: 218 cases, two optional fixture cases skipped in the ordinary run. The three new Swift pin-order tests were separately run with fixtures enabled, with no skips/failures. Rust-generated pinned projects in all eight categories were read/unpinned/written by Swift and re-read by Rust; package data and full metadata match, allowing only the intended pin changes and Swift’s normal modifiedAt update. Binary assets, links, Unicode/CRLF source, foreign maxUInt64 metadata, hidden note/snippet records and unknown fields are retained. The Swift suite also exposed a pre-existing CRLF append bug caused by Character-level hasSuffix("\n"); changed the byte-ending check so independent Shelf tasks append contiguously and replaced the mirrored expected-value calculation with explicit LF/CRLF fixtures. Both formerly conflicting tests now pass.

Native Mac QA: opened isolated Native Shelf Moved Tasks.fountage, moved the second normal checklist up, and Undo restored the original order/clean checkpoint. Pin/Unpin returned it to its base position. Tab/Tab/Return pinned the first checklist; pinned both, moved the second pin above the first, and saved Native Checklist Pins.fountage. Unpinning both after that rearrangement restored normal order; Undo returned to the exact saved pinned layout and cleared Edited. Redo/Undo retained the expected states; native Open Project reopened the saved pinned group/order cleanly with fresh history and unchanged task counts/content. Focus stayed on Done after each structural action. Package comparison proves only the two pinned flags and the new pinnedOrder changed; normal order, tasks, manuscript, all other metadata, attachments and links remain exact. Four matching complete native recovery sessions and all-eight-category Rust/Swift evidence are archived in output/slint-checklist-management-20261015/verification.json. Clean saved QA project left on Shelf; no user manuscript edited.
